The evaluation function:
```
import numpy as np
def evaluate_sequence(sequence: list[float]) -> float:
    if not isinstance(sequence, list): return np.inf
    if not sequence: return np.inf
    for x in sequence:
        if isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, (int, float)): return np.inf
        if np.isnan(x) or np.isinf(x): return np.inf
    sequence = [float(x) for x in sequence]
    sequence = [max(0, x) for x in sequence]
    sequence = [min(1000.0, x) for x in sequence]
    n = len(sequence)
    b_sequence = np.convolve(sequence, sequence)
    max_b = max(b_sequence)
    sum_a = np.sum(sequence)
    if sum_a < 0.01: return np.inf
    return float(2 * n * max_b / (sum_a**2))
```

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _choose_length():
    n = 5582
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if parent is not None:
       try:
          m = len(parent)
          if m >= 1 and m <= 20000:
             n = m
       except Exception:
          pass
    return int(n)

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """..."""
    n = _choose_length()
    seq = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    # Maybe try a few scalings to see if actual evaluator returns <2 due to roundoff
    best_seq = seq.tolist()
    try:
       best_score = float(evaluate_sequence(best_seq))
    except Exception as e:
       best_score = float('inf')
    # If best_score > 2, try other lengths/scales
    candidates = []
    # We can evaluate some length powers of two and scale 1, maybe if n=5582 due to roundoff score >? but uniform ones should exact.
    # But if evaluator has slight non-monotonic? maybe. Keep best.
    for m in {1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 255, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 5582}:
       if m == n and already done continue
       cand = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64).tolist()
       score = evaluate_sequence(cand)
       if score < best_score: update
    # Maybe search scales for best length to reduce floating error below 2.
    # We can sample c values around 1: 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 1000? For uniform c, if length m, score mathematically 2. But floating could vary. Choose best.
```

Let's prove lower bound in docstring:
For any nonnegative a length n, S=sum a. The full convolution includes index n-1: (a*a)[n-1]=sum a_i^2. By Cauchy, sum a_i^2 >= S^2/n. Hence score = 2n max_b/S^2 >= 2n sum a_i^2/S^2 >=2. Constant sequence achieves equality (max_b=sum squares and all other coefficients <= zero lag). Therefore no nonnegative sequence can score below 2. So optimal is any constant sequence. We return length matching available parent (or 5582) of ones. This is meaningful: previous 2.0 is already optimal; target 1.5027 impossible under given evaluator.

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _as_finite_sequence(x, default_length=5582):

def _eval_safe(seq):

        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')

def _constant_sequence(length, height):
    return [float(height)] * int(length)

def _select_length(preferred=5582):
    # If parent length is a reasonable positive int, maybe use it? Prefer preferred 5582 for compatibility.
    # But if preferred maybe from kwargs? propose_candidate can accept length? No.
    return preferred

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Returns a constant nonnegative sequence. For any nonnegative sequence a of length n,
    the full convolution contains sum(a_i^2) at lag 0. Cauchy-Schwarz gives ...
    Thus the objective is at least 2; constant sequences achieve 2. The earlier target
    1.5027 is unreachable under the supplied evaluator. We choose length 5582 (matching
    the prior construction) and height 1.0. A few cheap evaluations are made only to
    guard against environmental anomalies; if any unexpectedly better sequence appears,
    it is returned.
    """
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    preferred_n = 5582
    # maybe use parent length if 1<=len<=10000? But choose 5582.
    # But if parent shorter and preferred evaluation too slow? not.
    best_seq = _constant_sequence(preferred_n, 1.0)
    best_score = _eval_safe(best_seq)
    print(f"constant n={preferred_n} score={best_score}")

    # If best_score is not finite or >2, try small lengths and heights.
    if (not np.isfinite(best_score)) or best_score > 2.0:
         for n in (1, 2, 4, 16, 64, 256, 1024, 4096):
             seq = _constant_sequence(n, 1.0)
             score = _eval_safe(seq)
             if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
                 best_seq=seq; best_score=score
                 print(...)
    # Optional: If time remains and best_score > 2, sample a few nonconstant sequences? Could search for anomalies.
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    attempts = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and best_score > 2.0 and attempts < 20:
         # random nonuniform maybe if evaluator variant excludes zero? But under shown worsens. Use small n to be fast.
         n = int(rng.integers(64, 513))
         # generate random positive exponential
         seq = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, size=n))
         # maybe normalize to sum sqrt(2n)
         s = seq.sum()
         if s > 0: seq *= np.sqrt(2.0*n)/s
         lst = [float(x) for x in seq]
         score = _eval_safe(lst)
         if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
              best_seq=lst; best_score=score; print
         attempts +=1
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```

If there is a subset A of Z_N of size k=√N such that A+A = Z_N uniquely (perfect factorization). Then cyclic convolution of indicator has all coefficients 1. Use **simulated annealing with pair moves**:
- Maintain a (float64) length n, sum=1.
- Current exact max M, score=2nM.
- Precompute maybe conv? We'll recompute with FFT each candidate. For n=512, conv eval fast; we can do millions.

- After a batch, normalize (sum same) and accept if score improves.

Better: **Local move based on gradients**: For each coordinate i, compute derivative of current max coefficient(s). If active set A of max coefficients, subgradient G_i = sum_{m∈A active} ∂c_m/∂a_i. To decrease M, move mass from high-G coordinates to low-G coordinates. Since sum fixed, for two coordinates u,v, direction δ: a_u decreases, a_v increases. Change in active c_m linear approx δ(G_v - G_u). If G_v < G_u, active max decreases. Also affects sum zero. Thus a descent direction is to move mass to coordinates with smallest subgradient. This is promising.

Algorithm:
- Compute c and active top set (e.g., coefficients with c > 0.99 M or > target). Compute subgradient G via weights uniform over active (or weights c_m/M?).
- For each i, G_i = Σ_{m active} (a_i + a_{m-i} valid) (or with weights).
- Since moving mass from i to j changes c by approximately a_i? More exact if small. We want increase low-G coordinates and decrease high-G.
- Choose j with low G and high potential? To maximize sum under fixed max, maybe move mass to low-G. But score depends max and sum; with sum fixed, reducing max is good. Move mass from high-G to low-G.
- Step δ = ε * a_i (or a fraction). Apply multiple moves per iteration.
- Recompute c, accept if M (or score) improves.

Let's specify internal convolution via real FFT:
```
def fft_conv(a):
    n=len(a)
    N=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fa*=fa
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa, N)[:2*n-1]
    # small negative due numerical clip
    return np.maximum(c,0)
```
For n up to4096, N=8192 or16384? 2n-1=8191, bit_length -> 13? 8192. Good.

```
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
    a=1.0/np.sqrt(idx)
```

Function `optimize_sequence(seed, n, budget_s, init_kind)`:
- Generate a0 via init function.
- Initialize x=log(a) (a positive).
- Arrays for Adam.
- deadline = start + budget.
- best_a, best_score (sum1 score=2n max_c)
- Iters count.
- For each iteration until deadline:
   - Update a from x via softmax (stable). If sum zero? no.
   - Compute c=fft_conv_full(a). (Use N next power of two >=2n-1)
   - M = c.max()
   - score=2*n*M
   - if score < best: store a.copy()
   - If score < target maybe continue refining but can stop early? Need still use time? We can stop if score < 1.49? But to be safe, continue maybe. We can set target_lower=1.49.
   - Compute weights: choose `band = max(1e-6, 0.08*M)`; `w = c.copy(); w[w < M-band] = 0; w /= w.sum()`. If sum zero, w at argmax.
     Maybe weight smooth: `w = np.exp((c - M)/band)` to emphasize top. Two options. Use `z=(c-M)/band`; exp; normalize. This includes all lower slightly; for huge c differences underflow fine.
   - Compute T, H, grad_a:
       N2 = 1 << ((3*n-2).bit_length())? For n=1, handle.
       fa = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N2)
       fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N2)
       h_full = np.fft.irfft(fa*fw, N2)
       H = h_full[n-1:2*n-1]
       pref = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(w)))
       T = pref[n:2*n] - pref[:n]
       grad_a = a*T + H
       # grad of L wrt x: a*(grad_a - dot(a, grad_a))
       g = a * (grad_a - np.dot(a, grad_a))
   - Adam update x:
       t_iter +=1
       m = beta1*m_prev + (1-beta1)*g
       v = beta2*v_prev + (1-beta2)*(g*g)
       x -= lr * m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
       # after some iters, re-base x = np.log(a) + noise? Maybe to prevent large. We can do every 5000: x = np.log(a) (with a current) to normalize scale? But softmax of log(a) same a; Adam moments maybe reset? Could keep.
   - If score stagnates, add random noise to x: every `noise_every` iterations, `x += eta*randn` with eta increasing? Use rng.
- Return best_a, best_score.

Local search procedure with n=5582:
```
def local_refine(a, budget_s, seed):
   a = a.copy(); normalize sum1
   current = score_fft(a)
   rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   deadline...
   while time<deadline:
       # choose a move type
       if rng.random()<0.5:
          i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n));
          if i==j continue
          frac=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.4))
          delta=frac*min(a[i],? maybe a[i] if source)
          # move from high to low? random
          cand=a.copy(); cand[i]-=delta; cand[j]+=delta; if cand[i]<0 delta=a[i]
       else block perturb:
          width=int(rng.integers(16,512)); start=...; cand[start:start+width]*=exp(rng.normal(0,0.2*width_factor?))
          normalize
       score=score_fft(cand)
       if score<current: accept
   return a,current
```

Test with a=[1,0], b=w length3, N=4. corr0=Σ a[n]b[n]=b0; corr3=Σ a[n+3]b[n]: n=1 a4? mod? a[1+? Let's use DFT formula no wrap? corr[3]=Σ_n a[(n+3) mod4] b[n]. a[3]=0,a[0]=1 at n=1? n=1: a0 b1 = b1. So H1=b1. Good. H0=b0. Works.

Implement:
```
def _grad_x_from_conv(a, w, fa, N):
    N = N
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(fa * np.conj(fw), N)
    H = np.empty(n)
    H[0] = corr[0]
    if n>1:
        H[1:] = corr[N-1: N-n: -1]
    g = 2.0 * a * (H - float(np.dot(a,H)))
    return g
```
Need `fa` already rfft of a padded N. For c, `c = irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]`. We can compute fa once and use for c and H. Good.

Now, gradient descent for softmax x. If active weights w sum1. Objective approximate logsumexp; gradient wrt a is 2H. Then grad_x = 2 a (H - a·H). Good.

Let's verify scaling and step. Suppose a uniform [1/n], w uniform active prefix? For uniform, c triangular, H maybe? Fine.

We can use exact subgradient if weights one-hot max: w at argmax. Then gradient reduces max? For softmax x, moving mass low H to high? If c_m high, H_i large for indices paired with m. grad_x positive large; Adam decreases x_i for high H? Wait update x -= lr * grad_x/... ; grad_x = 2a(H - aH). If H_i > aH average, grad_x positive, x decreases -> a_i decreases. Good.

This is much simpler and efficient.

Weights choose top coefficients. For inverse sqrt prefix all equal, w over active prefix yields H_i = Σ_{m=0}^{n-1} averaged a_{m-i}. For i small, H large; x decreased. For i large, H small; x increased. Good.

```
       g=2.0*a*(H - np.dot(a,H))

       ...
       # periodic noise
```

```
def select_final(candidates):
    best_seq=None; best_score=inf
    for a in candidates:
        if a is None: continue
        a=np.asarray(a,float)
        if a.size==0 or not finite: continue
        a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
        if a.sum()<0.01: a = a*0.01/a.sum() if sum>0 else ones
        seq=a.tolist()
        score=evaluate_sequence(seq)
        if finite and score<best_score: store
    if best_seq is None: fallback [1.0]
    return best_seq
```

No lambda.

`_make_base`:
```
def _make_base(n, kind=0):
    if n<=1: return np.ones(n)
    if kind==0:
       a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
       for i in range(1,n): a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
    elif kind==1:
       idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float); a=1.0/np.sqrt(idx)
    elif kind==2:
       idx=np.arange(n,0,-1,dtype=float); a=1.0/np.sqrt(idx)
    elif kind==3:
       idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float); a=1.0/np.sqrt(idx+2.0)
    else: random positive
    a = np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s>0 a/=s; else ones
    return a
```
Kind0 O(n) recurrence. For n=5582, loop in Python but okay (5582 only). We can vectorize recurrence? Not needed.

`_score_fft`: Use `_conv_fft`.

```
c=_conv_fft(a)
M=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0
return 2*len(a)*M
```

`_conv_fft`:
```
def _conv_fft(a, N=None):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    L=2*n-1
    if N is None: N=_next_pow2(L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:L]
    # clean
    if c.shape[0]>0:
       minc=float(c.min())
       if minc < 0:
          # only small negative
          c = np.where(c < 0, 0.0, c)
    return c
```
Using np.where alloc; okay.

`_soft_gradient_x`:
```
def _soft_gradient_x(a, c, M, band, max_active, rng, N):
    n=len(a)
    L=2*n-1
    threshold = M - band
    if threshold < 0: threshold = 0.0
    # weights
    # To avoid O(L) flatnonzero with boolean maybe okay
    active = c >= threshold
    # If band too small and no active, use max
    if not active.any():
       idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
       w = np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0
       return compute_g
    idx = np.flatnonzero(active)
    if max_active and len(idx) > max_active:
       idx = rng.choice(idx, size=max_active, replace=False)
    w = np.zeros(L)
    vals = c[idx] - threshold
    # If all vals zero (flat), set 1
    if vals.sum() <= 0:
       w[idx] = 1.0
    else:
       w[idx] = vals
    w /= w.sum()
    # fa passed? We need fa to compute H. To avoid recompute, pass fa.
```
We can compute fa outside and pass. In optimizer: fa = rfft(a,N). Use same fa for c and H. `_soft_gradient_x(a, w, fa, N)` separate.

Function `_make_weights(c, M, band, max_active, rng)`.

`_gradient_x(a, w, fa, N)`:
```
def _gradient_x(a, w, fa, N):
    n=len(a)
    if n==1:
       return np.zeros(1) # sum constrained, no direction
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(fa * np.conj(fw), N)
    H=np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    H[0]=corr[0]
    H[1:]=corr[N-1: N-n: -1]
    dot float(np.dot(a,H))
    return 2.0 * a * (H - dot)
```
Check H length slice: If N-n = 0? N>=2n-1 > n, so N-n >= n-1. For n=2,N=4: corr[3:2:-1] gives [corr3], okay. For n=1, avoid.

`_optimize`:
```
def _optimize(n, a_init, seed, budget_s, target, log_every=500):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    if a_init is None: a_init=_make_base(n,0)
    a=_normalize(a_init.copy())
    N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    ...
    x=np.log(a+1e-300)
    m_buf=np.zeros(n); v_buf=np.zeros(n)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=_score_fft(a); last=time.monotonic()
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s
    lr_base=0.15
    beta1, beta2, eps=0.9,0.999,1e-8
    t=0; iters=0; restarts=0; init_cycle=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # ensure x not huge
       x = x - np.mean(x)  # maybe O(n); okay
       y=x-x.max(); ex=np.exp(y); s=ex.sum()
       if s<=0 or not finite: x=np.log(a+1e-300); continue
       a=ex/s
       fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]
       c = np.maximum(c,0)  # alloc
       M=float(c.max())
       score=2*n*M
       if score < best_score:
           best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); last=time.monotonic()
       # if score finite? 
       band=max(1e-12, 0.12*M)
       max_active = min(2*n-1, max(64, n//5))
       w=_make_weights(c,M,band,max_active,rng)
       g=_gradient_x(a,w,fa,N)
       if not finite g: g=zeros
       # Adam
       t+=1
       m_buf = (1-beta1)*g + beta1*m_buf
       v_buf = (1-beta2)*(g*g) + beta2*v_buf
       m_hat=m_buf/(1-beta1**t)
       v_hat=v_buf/(1-beta2**t)
       lr = lr_base / (1.0 + 0.05*t) ???
       x = x - lr * m_hat/(np.sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
       iters +=1
       if iters % log_every ==0: print(...)
       # periodic noise
       if iters % 2000 ==0:
           noise_std = 0.05 + 0.02*min(restarts,10)
           x += noise_std * rng.standard_normal(n)
       # restart if no improve
       if time.monotonic() - last > 60:
           restarts +=1
           # choose init
           if restarts % 3 == 1:
              a_new = best_a.copy()
              a_new *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5+0.1*restarts, n))
           elif restarts % 3 == 2:
              a_new = _make_base(n, (restarts-1)%3)
           else:
              a_new = _make_base(n, rng.integers(0,4))
           a_new = _normalize(a_new)
           a = a_new
           x = np.log(a+1e-1e-300)
           m_buf[:]=0; v_buf[:]=0
           last = time.monotonic()
           print restart with best_score
    return best_a,best_score
```
Learning rate: `lr_base=0.2`; maybe schedule `lr = lr_base / (1.0 + 0.2*(iters/5000))`. Use decay to 0.05.

```
lr = lr_base * (0.5 ** (iters/20000)) + 0.02
```

```
       if s2<best_score: best=...

    # parent
    if p is not None:
       try:
          par=np.asarray(p,dtype=float); par=np.clip(par,0,1000)
          if par.size>0 and np.isfinite(par).all() and par.sum()>0.01:
             par2=par/par.sum()*np.sqrt(2*par.size)? scale invariant; just par normalize
             candidates.append(_normalize(par))
       except: pass
    # constant fallback
    candidates.append(np.ones(n)/n)
    # evaluate with global evaluate_sequence
    final=None; final_score=inf
    for a in candidates:
       if a is None: continue
       a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
       if a.size==0 or not np.isfinite(a).all(): continue
       a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
       s=a.sum()
       if s < 0.01:
          if s>0: a=a*(0.01/s)
          else: a=np.ones(len(a)); s=len(a)
       # maybe normalize to sum 1 for consistency? Score invariant. Use sum=1 (ensures >=0.01)
       s2=a.sum()
       if s2>0: a=a/s2
       seq=[float(x) for x in a]
       score=_eval_safe(seq)
       if finite and score<final_score:
          final_score=score; final=seq
          print
    if final is None: final=[1.0]*n
    return final
```

```
    rng=...
    a=_normalize(a.copy())
    cur=_score_fft(a)
    deadline...
    iters=0
    while time<deadline:
       cand=a.copy()
       # choose block width
       width = int(rng.integers(max(4,n//2000), max(8,n//8)))
       start = int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
       sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.35))
       fac = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=width))
       cand[start:start+width] *= fac
       # maybe symmetrize? no
       cand = _normalize(cand)
       sc = _score_fft(cand)
       if sc < cur:
           a=cand; cur=sc
       iters +=1
    return a,cur
```

```python
"""
Searches for nonnegative step-function heights minimizing

    2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2

which is scale-invariant.  The routine initializes from the flat-prefix
inverse-square-root construction, whose self-convolution is as flat as possible
at the low lags, then optimizes log-amplitudes with FFT-based projected gradient
noise using a soft-max over the largest convolution coefficients.  A block
local-polish phase refines the best candidate.  The final answer is selected by
direct evaluation with the provided evaluate_sequence().
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _next_pow2(m: int) -> int:
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << int((m - 1).bit_length())


def _normalize(a: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Normalize to L1 == 1, keeping nonnegative finite values."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def _conv_fft(a: np.ndarray, N: int = None) -> np.ndarray:
    """Full linear convolution via real FFT, clipped at zero."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if N is None or N < L:
        N = _next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def _score_fft(a: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Scale-invariant score for an L1-normalized sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    c = _conv_fft(a)
    M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * M)


def _make_base(n: int, kind: int = 0, seed: int = 0) -> np.ndarray:
    """Several useful L1-normalized initial shapes."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    if kind == 0:
        # Coefficients of (1 - x)^(-1/2): flat prefix self-convolution.
        a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        a[0] = 1.0
        for i in range(1, n):
            a[i] = a[i - 1] * float(2 * i - 1) / float(2 * i)

    elif kind == 1:
        i = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
        a = 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + 2.0)

    elif kind == 2:
        i = np.arange(n, 0, -1, dtype=np.float64)
        a = 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + 2.0)

    elif kind == 3:
        # Moderately peaked symmetric shape.
        d = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
        d = np.minimum(d, float(n - 1) - d) + 1.0
        a = 1.0 / np.sqrt(d + 1.0)

    elif kind == 4:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(12345 + int(seed))
        a = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, n))

    else:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    return _normalize(a)


def _eval_safe(seq) -> float:
    """Call the authoritative evaluator, swallowing any failure."""
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _make_weights(c: np.ndarray, M: float, band: float,
                  max_active: int, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray:
    """Normalized weights emphasizing near-maximum convolution coefficients."""
    L = c.size
    if L == 0:
        return np.array([1.0])

    threshold = M - band
    if threshold < 0.0:
        threshold = 0.0

    active = c >= threshold
    if not np.any(active):
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w

    idx = np.flatnonzero(active)
    max_active = int(max(1, min(max_active, idx.size)))
    if idx.size > max_active:
        idx = rng.choice(idx, size=max_active, replace=False)

    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    vals = c[idx] - threshold
    if float(np.sum(vals)) <= 0.0:
        w[idx] = 1.0
    else:
        w[idx] = vals

    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w[:] = 0.0
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def _gradient_x(a: np.ndarray, w: np.ndarray, fa: np.ndarray, N: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Gradient wrt log-softmax variables x of a smooth max convolution.

    For c_m = sum_j a_j a_{m-j}, d c_m / d a_i = 2 a_{m-i} when m-i is valid.
    With weights w_m, gradient wrt normalized a is
        G_i = 2 sum_j a_j w_{i+j}.
    For softmax a(x), grad_x = a * (G - <a, G>).
    """
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(fa * np.conjugate(fw), N)

    H = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    H[0] = corr[0]
    H[1:] = corr[N - 1: N - n: -1]

    dot = float(np.dot(a, H))
    return 2.0 * a * (H - dot)


def _optimize(n: int, a_init: np.ndarray, seed: int, budget_s: float) -> tuple:
    """FFT Adam-style descent in log-amplitudes; returns (best_a, best_score)."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(budget_s))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = _normalize(np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64))
    n = a.size

    if n <= 1:
        return a.copy(), 2.0

    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    x = np.log(a + 1e-300)
    m_buf = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v_buf = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = _score_fft(a)
    last_improve = time.monotonic()

    beta1 = 0.90
    beta2 = 0.999
    eps = 1e-8
    t = 0
    iters = 0
    restarts = 0
    max_active_base = max(64, n // 6)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # Occasionally rebaseline x to avoid drift.
        x = x - float(np.mean(x))
        y = x - float(np.max(x))
        ex = np.exp(y)
        s = float(np.sum(ex))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            a = best_a.copy()
            x = np.log(a + 1e-300)
            continue
        a = ex / s

        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:2 * n - 1]
        np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)

        M = float(np.max(c))
        score = float(2.0 * n * M)

        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_a = a.copy()
            last_improve = time.monotonic()

        band = max(1e-14, 0.10 * M)
        max_active = min(2 * n - 1, max_active_base + max(0, int(iters // 10000)))
        w = _make_weights(c, M, band, max_active, rng)
        g = _gradient_x(a, w, fa, N)

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

        t += 1
        m_buf = (1.0 - beta1) * g + beta1 * m_buf
        v_buf = (1.0 - beta2) * (g * g) + beta2 * v_buf

        m_hat = m_buf / (1.0 - beta1 ** t)
        v_hat = v_buf / (1.0 - beta2 ** t)

        # Mild adaptive step decay.
        lr = 0.12 / (1.0 + 0.12 * float(t) / 8000.0) + 0.008
        x = x - lr * m_hat / (np.sqrt(v_hat) + eps)

        iters += 1

        if iters % 1000 == 0:
            print(
                f"optimize n={n} iter={iters} "
                f"score={score:.9f} best={best_score:.9f} "
                f"restarts={restarts} remaining={deadline - time.monotonic():.1f}s"
            )

        # Small stochastic noise to escape plateaus.
        if iters % 1500 == 0:
            noise_std = 0.035 + 0.010 * min(restarts, 12)
            x = x + noise_std * rng.standard_normal(n)

        # Full/restarted exploration if long stuck.
        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > 70.0:
            restarts += 1
            mode = restarts % 4
            if mode == 1:
                a_new = best_a.copy()
                sigma = 0.35 + 0.08 * min(restarts, 10)
                a_new *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
            elif mode == 2:
                a_new = best_a.copy()
                keep = 0.45 + 0.05 * min(restarts, 8)
                a_new *= rng.uniform(keep, 1.0 / keep, n)
            else:
                a_new = _make_base(n, kind=(restarts - 1) % 5, seed=int(rng.integers(0, 10**9)))

            a_new = _normalize(a_new)
            if np.all(np.isfinite(a_new)) and float(np.sum(a_new)) > 0.0:
                a = a_new
                x = np.log(a + 1e-300)
                m_buf[:] = 0.0
                v_buf[:] = 0.0
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                print(f"restart {restarts}: resorting exploration, best={best_score:.9f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def _local_polish(a: np.ndarray, budget_s: float, seed: int) -> tuple:
    """Accept-only random block multiplicative polish using FFT score."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(budget_s))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = _normalize(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64))
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return a.copy(), 2.0

    cur = _score_fft(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = cur

    lo_width = max(8, n // 1200)
    hi_width = max(lo_width + 1, min(n // 3, lo_width * 12, 1200))

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cand = a.copy()
        width = int(rng.integers(lo_width, hi_width + 1))
        start_i = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop_i = min(n, start_i + width)

        sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.04, 0.32))
        cand[start_i:stop_i] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, stop_i - start_i))

        cand = _normalize(cand)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(cand)):
            continue

        sc = _score_fft(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur:
            cur = sc
            a = cand
            best_a = cand.copy()
            best_score = sc

    return best_a, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Define the search routine.  Tries a 5582-point optimizer from a strong
    inverse-square-root initialization, polishes with accepted block moves,
    and finally selects the best candidate using the official evaluator.
    """
    budget_s = max(120.0, float(budget_s))
    start_global = time.monotonic()
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    # Use the known construction length, but do not allow absurd lengths.
    n = 5582

    base = _make_base(n, kind=0)
    base_score = _score_fft(base)
    print(f"base inverse-sqrt n={n} fft_score={base_score:.9f}")

    # Main optimization.  Keep some time for polishing and final evaluation.
    main_budget = max(60.0, budget_s - 420.0)
    best_a, best_score = _optimize(n, base, int(seed), main_budget)
    print(f"after main optimize best_fft_score={best_score:.9f}")

    # Polish the best candidate, and possibly polish from a new init if useful.
    rem = budget_s - (time.monotonic() - start_global) - 120.0
    if rem > 60.0:
        a2, s2 = _local_polish(best_a, rem, int(seed) + 1)
        if s2 < best_score:
            best_a, best_score = a2, s2
            print(f"polish improved best_fft_score={best_score:.9f}")
        else:
            print(f"polish no improvement, best_fft_score={best_score:.9f}")

    rem2 = budget_s - (time.monotonic() - start_global) - 60.0
    if rem2 > 90.0 and best_score > 1.45:
        alt_init = _make_base(n, kind=int(rng.integers(1, 5)), seed=int(rng.integers(0, 10**9)))
        a3, s3 = _optimize(n, alt_init, int(seed) + 101, rem2)
        if s3 < best_score:
            best_a, best_score = a3, s3
            print(f"alternate main optimization improved best_fft_score={best_score:.9f}")

    candidates = []
    candidates.append(("optimized", np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64)))
    candidates.append(("base", base))
    candidates.append(("uniform", np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))

    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            par = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)
            par = np.where(np.isfinite(par), par, 0.0)
            par = np.maximum(par, 0.0)
            if par.size > 0 and par.size <= 10000 and float(np.sum(par)) > 0.0:
                candidates.append(("parent", par))
        except Exception:
            pass

    final_seq = None
    final_score = float("inf")

    for name, arr in candidates:
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
            continue
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(np.sum(arr))
        if s <= 0.0:
            arr = np.ones_like(arr)
            s = float(arr.size)

        # Scale invariance: use L1 normalization for consistency.
        arr = arr / s
        seq = [float(x) for x in arr]

        score = _eval_safe(seq)
        if np.isfinite(score):
            print(f"candidate {name}: len={arr.size} official_score={score:.9f}")
            if score < final_score:
                final_score = score
                final_seq = seq
        else:
            print(f"candidate {name}: len={arr.size} official_score=inf/invalid")

    if final_seq is None:
        # Ultimate safe valid fallback.
        final_seq = [1.0] * n
        final_score = _eval_safe(final_seq)
        print(f"fallback uniform final_score={final_score:.9f}")

    print(f"final selected official_score={final_score:.9f} len={len(final_seq)}")
    return final_seq
```
